The artifact count is wrong: `edits.py` defines 45 edits, and `TABLE.txt` has 46 cases including the unchanged control—not 47. The frozen classifier also treats a method reference followed by a separate parenthesized statement as a call because it discards statement-ending newlines. I’m checking that case against the readiness counters. Bash cannot start in this sandbox, so shell-dependent results will be marked as static analysis.

import ast, hashlib, io, pathlib, re, subprocess, types
repo='GXEMUL-SEC'
def git(*args): return subprocess.check_output(['git','-C',repo,*args])
def read_py(rev):
    records=[]
    for record in git('ls-tree','-rz',rev,'--','regress').split(b'\\0'):
        if not record: continue
        meta,path=record.split(b'\\t',1); path=path.decode('utf-8')
        if path.endswith('.py') and not path.startswith('regress/artifacts/'):
            records.append((meta.split()[2],path.removeprefix('regress/')))
    data=git('cat-file','--batch') if not records else subprocess.check_output(['git','-C',repo,'cat-file','--batch'],input=b'\\n'.join(sha for sha,_ in records)+b'\\n')
    pos=0; result={}
    for _,name in records:
        end=data.index(b'\\n',pos); header=data[pos:end].split(); size=int(header[2]); pos=end+1
        result[name]=data[pos:pos+size].decode('utf-8'); pos+=size+1
    return result

VERDICT: DO NOT SHIP -- the token classifier introduces a new false pass, the ruling writer can emit uncommented text, and several claimed record corrections remain incomplete.

1. **Q1 — Verify the repair’s claims.**

   **(a) The seven advertised shapes mostly work, but tokenization does not establish a call.**

   | Shape tested against the frozen classifier | Result |
   |---|---|
   | Trailing comment containing the predicate | Excluded |
   | Ordinary string containing the predicate | Excluded |
   | Docstring containing the predicate | Excluded |
   | Spaces, tabs, continued lines, intervening comments | Recognized |
   | Single and double quotes | Recognized |
   | Two whole-buffer occurrences on one line | Counted as two |
   | Unreadable file, invalid UTF-8, unclosed triple quote or parenthesis | Listed promptless |

   The defect is [discarding `tokenize.NEWLINE`](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c2_frozen/census_tokens.py:31). Unlike `NL` inside a continued expression, `NEWLINE` terminates a statement. Removing it manufactures apparent calls from separate statements. Q2 demonstrates a new false pass.

   Separately, `def endswith (arg):` satisfies `has_ends_with_call`. The documented NAME-plus-parenthesis rule describes that behavior accurately; calling its result a “code-level call” does not.

   **(b) Refusals, enumerated from the frozen deriver:**

   | Diagnostic / status | Every distinct printed refusal |
   |---|---|
   | `REFUSED`, rc 2 | Ruling fails date/reason format; ruling contains a forbidden character |
   | `REFUSED`, rc 4 | No grandfather/release names; duplicate argument; already grandfathered; requested grandfather name is not NEW; requested release name is not GONE |
   | `REFUSED`, rc 5 | Converted counts disagree; NEW promptless names remain; GONE grandfathered names remain; bare count rises; unknown count rises; a change requiring a ruling has none |
   | `DERIVE_FAIL`, rc 2 | No working Python; missing gate |
   | `DERIVE_FAIL`, rc 3 | Missing enumerator function; missing enumerator assignment; denominator is not positive |
   | `DERIVE_FAIL`, rc 6 | Writer fails |
   | Usage, rc 2 | Extra `--write` arguments; unrecognized initial option |

   That is **13 `REFUSED` branches, six `DERIVE_FAIL` branches, and two usage branches**.

   The header promises nine refusal conditions: zero denominator; converted-count disagreement; NEW names; GONE names; bare growth; unknown growth; converted-count decline without a ruling; whole-count change without a ruling; whole-file-map change without a ruling. All nine have code. The additional argument and environment diagnostics are reasonable.

   **G5 nevertheless is not closed.** The [ruling validator](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c2_frozen/derive_hygiene_pins.sh:55) applies a line-oriented regular expression to potentially multiline input. One qualifying line suffices, and its forbidden-character check permits newlines. Running the extracted Python writer in memory confirmed that subsequent ruling lines are emitted without `#`, becoming shell source rather than commentary. Reject CR/LF before writing, or explicitly encode every line as a comment.

   Also, the claimed prohibition on “quotes” only prohibits double quotes; apostrophes pass. Describe the actual restriction.

   | `n4a` | **A new promptless probe excluded from `_ptylist`** |

   The first nine and `n10` fall outside the relevant explicitly defined patterns. Extra parentheses add tokens; they are not merely spacing.

   The remaining three are **population omissions**. In particular, `n4a` contains no readiness test to respell. Their deferral may survive the stopping rule, but G12’s justification does not.

2. **Q2 — A new false pass.**

   The smallest valid counterexample I measured is a one-site rewrite of `arm_bdt_probe.py`’s allowlisted condition:

   ```python
   ready = self.buf.rstrip().endswith
   ("GXemul>")
   if ready:
       # existing body
   ```

   This takes a method reference, evaluates a separate string expression, and tests the method object’s truthiness. There is **no whole-buffer suffix call**.

   Measurements:

   - The modified probe parses successfully.
   - AST inspection confirms that the whole-buffer call disappeared.
   - The frozen token counter nevertheless reports **one** whole-buffer site.
   - The preceding F2 line matcher counts **zero**, so this false pass is introduced by the repair.
   - Preserving `NEWLINE` changes the token result to zero while retaining recognition of the seven spacing/quotation/continuation controls tested alongside it.

   Every affected readiness input remains unchanged under the frozen repair.    - **The sibling helper overclaim survives twice.** [Gate line 74](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c2_frozen/gate_hygiene.sh:74) and line 142 still say “any indirection through a helper” becomes unknown. `n3k/n3k2` directly refute that. Correct the attribution or supply the actual ruling.

   - **The new chronology is wrong.** [“Until the classifier … learned to read tokens”](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/census0923/c2_frozen/gate_hygiene.sh:411) credits this repair with exposing `crime_width`. The frozen F2 input already strips full-line comments and already includes that grandfathered name.

   - **“NUL-safe” requires qualification.** NUL-delimited discovery reaches `grep`, but `grep -l` then produces newline-delimited paths. The complete pipeline is not NUL-safe.

   - **The control denominator is wrong.** Enumerating decorators in [edits.py](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/hygienecensus_20260923/controls_f2/edits.py) gives **45 edits**. 5. **Q5 — Is deferring e9 defensible?**

   **Yes, under the explicit rule permitting pre-existing evasions to be filed.** “Outside a spelling census’s reach by construction” is not a defensible technical reason.

   Closing e9 is a bounded alternative: change the initial `py_code` filter and the `allends`, `bare`, and `full` patterns together, then check their occurrence-count relationship. Updating only the downstream patterns leaves the initial filter blind. Ordinary line-oriented whitespace matching would still not cover every multiline spelling.

   The missing disposition is therefore: **optionally close this specific spelling now, without claiming general semantic coverage**. Deferral remains acceptable after correcting all conflicting claims. It does not excuse the newly introduced Q2 defect.

   - **The control denominator is wrong.** Enumerating decorators in [edits.py](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/hygienecensus_20260923/controls_f2/edits.py) gives **45 edits**. Both `TABLE.txt` and `SUMMARY.txt` enumerate **46 cases including `real`**, not 47. The thirteen named surviving evasions are indeed thirteen.

   - **The earlier total is also wrong.** Combining the authoritative prior `runs2`, `runs3`, and `runs4` tables gives **34 PASS edits**, or **32 after excluding the separately identified `n5b` and `n7` doors**: seven old cases plus **25**, not 24, new cases.

   The crime comment’s eight-operation claim checks out: `ld`, `ldP`, `lw`, `lwP`, `sd`, `sdP`, `sw`, `swP`. The grandfathered list contains 43 entries and 43 unique names.

   Another independently found, pre-existing residual: the release writer’s boundary regex matches both `foo.py` and the suffix of `pref-foo.py`. Releasing the former therefore hits its assertion. File this under the stopping rule; adding `/` to the boundary class does not establish exact-name matching.

5. **Q5 — Is deferring e9 defensible?**

   **Yes, under the explicit rule permitting pre-existing evasions to be filed.** “Outside a spelling census’s reach by construction” is not a defensible technical reason.

   Closing e9 is a bounded alternative: change the initial `py_code` filter and the `allends`, `bare`, and `full` patterns together, then check their occurrence-count relationship. Updating only the downstream patterns leaves the initial filter blind. Ordinary line-oriented whitespace matching would still not cover every multiline spelling.

   The missing disposition is therefore: **optionally close this specific spelling now, without claiming general semantic coverage**. Deferral remains acceptable after correcting all conflicting claims. It does not excuse the newly introduced Q2 defect.
